, as the indices must coincide for any configuration in the support of
\Delta
.
Indeed, we will later prove that
\Delta\sim_{cs}\Theta
.

4.3.
Behavioural Assessment of Constrained Bisimilarity

In the following, we state some important properties of
\sim_{cs}
.
A first result recovers the linearity of the relation.
We then prove that
\sim_{cs}
is strictly coarser than the previously defined
\sim_{s}
.
Furthermore, we show that our constraints are strong enough to agree with the limitations of the observational power prescribed by quantum theory.
More precisely, we consider the equivalence classes over distributions of quantum states implicitly represented by density operators, and we generalize them
as classes of bisimilar distributions of lqCCS configurations.
An undue curbing of contexts is not better than a deficient one,
therefore, we finally prove that the expressivity of the non-deterministic sum of processes is preserved
when justified by classical information.

Theorem 4.4.

If
\Delta_{i}\sim_{cs}\Theta_{i}
for
i=1,2
, then
\Delta_{1}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\Delta_{2}\sim_{cs}\Theta_{1}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\Theta_{2}

for any
p\in[0,1]
.

Proof sketch.

It follows from the linearity of barbs and the decomposability of
\longsquiggly_{\pi}
, which hold by definition.
∎

Regarding quantum properties, the enhanced semantics limits the capability of
the observer to perform different choices in different configurations of the
same distribution.
As a result, cs-bisimilarity is strictly broader than s-bisimilarity, as observers are less powerful.

Theorem 4.5.

\sim_{s}\ \subsetneq\ \sim_{cs}
.

Proof sketch.

4.6
shows that
\sim_{cs}\ \not\subseteq\ \sim_{s}
.
For
\sim_{s}\ \subseteq\ \sim_{cs}
we provide a translation
{\llparenthesis\,}{\,\cdot\,}{\,\rrparenthesis}
that annotates a given
R
with fresh barbs encoding the non-deterministic choices.
We prove by induction that the enhanced semantics of
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}

corresponds to the standard semantics of
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P||{\llparenthesis\,}R{\,\rrparenthesis}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}
, where
{\llparenthesis\,}R{\,\rrparenthesis}
is composed with the parallel operator (as required by
\sim_{s}
).
In particular,
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta
if and only if
{\llparenthesis\,}R{\,\rrparenthesis}\downarrow_{\pi}
and
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P||{\llparenthesis\,}R{\,\rrparenthesis}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Delta^{\prime}
with
\Delta^{\prime}\!\downarrow_{\pi}^{0}

(roughly, a
\longsquiggly_{\pi}
move corresponds to a
\rightarrow
one where the barb
\pi
is “consumed”).
This allows us to prove that
\sim_{s}
is a cs-bisimulation.
∎

From the proof above it follows that the enhanced semantics does not give cs-bisimilarity any additional discriminating power with respect to the standard semantics. In other words, we could have defined
\sim_{cs}
without changing the semantics, just requiring instead that contexts express their non-deterministic choices as barbs. The enhanced semantics — as well as the absence of congruence rules for parallel observers — is then just a convenient way to assign a name to each possible non-deterministic choice, which fits well with the SOS-style rules.

We discuss now how cs-bisimilarity deals with the issue presented in
subsection 4.1
.

5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.60036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.60036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{1/2}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-24.56538pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.60036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.60036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-9.90433pt{1/2}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{-}{-},c!q,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
.
As detailed in
subsection 4.1
, one would expect
\Delta^{\prime}\sim_{cs}\Theta^{\prime}
, as they send indistinguishable quantum states.
We prove that this is the case, in particular, because the process
c!q
is deterministic.

A distribution
\Delta
is
deterministic
if its evolution is fully probabilistic, i.e. if it evolves in a single distribution up to bisimilarity. For example, distributions without parallel operators and non-deterministic sums are trivially deterministic.

Definition 4.7 (Deterministic processes).

A set of distributions
\mathcal{A}
is deterministic if
\Delta\in\mathcal{A}
implies that for any
O[{\,\cdot\,}],\Delta^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}
, if
O[\Delta]\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta^{\prime}
and
O[\Delta]\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
then
\Delta^{\prime}\sim_{cs}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
and
\Delta^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{A}
.
A distribution
\Delta
is called deterministic if it is contained in a deterministic set.
A process
P
is deterministic if
\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
is deterministic for any state
\rho
.

As previously stated, mixed states represented by the same density operator are indistinguishable.
We recover an analogous result for
lqCCS.
Roughly, quantum states can be combined into a point distribution when paired with identical deterministic processes.

Theorem 4.8.

If
P
is deterministic, then for any
\rho,\sigma,p,R
,
\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\sigma,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\sim_{cs}\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\sigma,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}

where
\rho\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\sigma
is defined as the density operator
p\rho+(1-p)\sigma
.

Proof sketch.

For any deterministic
P
, we prove by structural induction that

\mathcal{R}=\{\,(\bot,\bot)\,\}\cup\left\{\left(\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\sigma,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\ ,\ \overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\sigma,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\right)\mid\rho,\sigma,p,R\ \right\}

is a bisimulation up to convex hull
(
Bonchi et al. 2017
)
and up to bisimilarity (the soundness of which is given by
4.19
and according to
Pous and Sangiorgi 2011
).

The result mainly follows from the fact that the classical components of the distributions are identical, while the quantum components are indistinguishable, as superoperators and measurements are convex, i.e.
\mathcal{F}(\rho)\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\mathcal{F}(\sigma)=\mathcal{F}(\rho\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\sigma)
for any superoperator or measurement
\mathcal{F}
.
The hypothesis of determinism is required for the cases of non-deterministic sums and parallel compositions.
Indeed,
\Delta=\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P\parallel Q,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\sigma,P\parallel Q,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
may evolve differently with the left and right component, e.g. choosing
P
in the left and
Q
in the right.
The hypothesis of the process being deterministic ensures that no information is leaked about
\rho
and
\sigma
,
and ensures that the choice of
\Delta
is irrelevant, allowing
\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\sigma,P,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
to replicate its move.
This is not possible in general, as shown in
4.10
.
∎

Remarkably, transitivity of
\sim_{cs}
suffices for proving the bisimilarity of deterministic processes paired with distributions of quantum states that are represented by the same density operator.
Note for example that
\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{0}{0},c!q}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-31.70206pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-15.5321pt{{1}/{2}}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-31.70206pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-15.5321pt{{1}/{2}}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-22.54318pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-10.99321pt{{1}/{2}}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-21.4543pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-9.90433pt{{1}/{2}}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{1}{1},c!q}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
and
\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{+}{+},c!q}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-31.70206pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-15.5321pt{{1}/{2}}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-31.70206pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-15.5321pt{{1}/{2}}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-22.54318pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-10.99321pt{{1}/{2}}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-21.4543pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-9.90433pt{{1}/{2}}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{-}{-},c!q}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
of
4.6
and
4.1

are both bisimilar to
\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\frac{1}{2}{I},c!q}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
.
More in general, the equivalence classes represented by density operators are lifted to lqCCS distributions, yielding the equivalence
\mathcal{R}\subseteq\ \sim_{cs}

relating
\Delta
and
\Theta
deterministic whenever

\sum_{\rho}\Delta\big(\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\big)\rho=\sum_{\rho}\Theta\big(\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\big)\rho
for all
P,R
.
Note also that, thanks to the linearity of
\sim_{cs}
, the property above is not limited to syntactically identical processes.

A consequence of
Theorem 4.8
is that
\sim_{cs}
is not decomposable, similarly to other proposals addressing the limitations of probabilistic bisimilarity
(
Feng and Ying 2015
;
Deng 2018
)
.

Corollary 4.9.

cs-bisimilarity is not a decomposable relation.

Proof sketch.

Take
\Delta=\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{0}{0},c!q}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-31.70206pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-15.5321pt{{1}/{2}}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-31.70206pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-15.5321pt{{1}/{2}}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-22.54318pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-10.99321pt{{1}/{2}}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-21.4543pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-9.90433pt{{1}/{2}}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{1}{1},c!q}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
and

\Theta=\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{+}{+},c!q}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-31.70206pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-15.5321pt{{1}/{2}}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-31.70206pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-15.5321pt{{1}/{2}}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-22.54318pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-10.99321pt{{1}/{2}}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-21.4543pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-9.90433pt{{1}/{2}}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{-}{-},c!q}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
.
Notice that
\Delta\sim_{cs}\Theta
by
Theorem 4.8
.
Then, for
\sim_{cs}
to be decomposable,
\Theta
should be equal to
\Theta_{1}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-31.70206pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-15.5321pt{{1}/{2}}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-31.70206pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-15.5321pt{{1}/{2}}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-22.54318pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-10.99321pt{{1}/{2}}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-21.4543pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-9.90433pt{{1}/{2}}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\Theta_{2}
for some
\Theta_{1}\sim_{cs}\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{0}{0},c!q}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
.
But since
\Theta_{1}
can only be either
\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{+}{+},c!q}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
,
\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{-}{-},c!q}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
or a combination of them,
\Theta_{1}\not\sim_{cs}\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{0}{0},c!q}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
as they send observably different quantum values.
∎

Theorem 4.8
targets deterministic processes because they represent fully defined physical processes, e.g., where all the choices are performed by boolean conditions.
Indeed, there is no reason to expect the property to hold for processes expressing non-determinism à la CCS, as it
does not encode any physical behaviour considered in quantum theory.
More in detail, an extension of this theorem for general processes can only hold with overly constrained non-deterministic sums, and it would contradict
Theorem 4.12
below, attesting to the preservation of the expressiveness of non-determinism in processes.
We show an example of a non-deterministic process, derived from the sixth row of
Table 1
, for which
Theorem 4.8
does not apply.

Example 4.10.

Consider the following pair of processes of the last line of
Table 1

\displaystyle\text{Set}_{\scriptscriptstyle\outerproduct{+}{+}}(q).M_{01}(q\rhd{}x).(c!q+d!q)\text{ and }\text{Set}_{\scriptscriptstyle\outerproduct{0}{0}}(q).M_{\pm}(q\rhd{}x).(c!q+d!q)

We will later show that the distributions
\Delta=\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{+}{+},M_{01}(q\rhd{}x).\textbf{if }x=0\textbf{ then }P\textbf{ else }Q,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
and
\Theta=\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{0}{0},M_{\pm}(q\rhd{}x).\textbf{if }x=0\textbf{ then }P\textbf{ else }Q,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
to which the two processes reduce are not bisimilar.

As discussed previously, this is expected as we want to restrict the non-determinism of observers only.
Non-deterministic sum is typically used in processes to model unspecified behaviour, to be instantiated in future refinements.
Thus, we do not want to constrain non-determinism to the point that
+
cannot replicate the behaviour of its refinements like boolean conditions.

We say that
P^{\prime}
refines
P
, if
P^{\prime}
can be obtained from
P
by substituting some occurrences of
Q+Q^{\prime}
with either
Q
,
Q^{\prime}
, or
\textbf{if }e\textbf{ then }Q\textbf{ else }Q^{\prime}
for an arbitrary
e
.

\displaystyle P^{\prime}\ \square\ Q^{\prime}\preceq P\ \square\ Q\lx@proof@logical@and P^{\prime}\preceq PQ^{\prime}\preceq Q\hskip 18.49988pt\mu.P^{\prime}\preceq\mu.PP^{\prime}\preceq P\hskip 18.49988ptP^{\prime}\preceq P+QP^{\prime}\preceq P\hskip 18.49988ptQ^{\prime}\preceq P+QQ^{\prime}\preceq Q

\displaystyle P^{\prime}\setminus c\preceq P\setminus cP^{\prime}\preceq P\hskip 18.49988pt\textbf{if }e\textbf{ then }P^{\prime}\textbf{ else }Q^{\prime}\preceq P+Q\lx@proof@logical@and P^{\prime}\preceq PQ^{\prime}\preceq Q\hskip 18.49988pt\textbf{if }e\textbf{ then }P^{\prime}\textbf{ else }Q^{\prime}\preceq\textbf{if }e\textbf{ then }P\textbf{ else }Q\lx@proof@logical@and P^{\prime}\preceq PQ^{\prime}\preceq Q

Figure 7.
Refinement relation over lqCCS processes.

Definition 4.11.

The refinement relation
P^{\prime}\preceq P
is the smallest reflexive relation satisfying the rules in
Figure 7
.
We say that
P^{\prime}
refines
P
, and that a configuration
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P^{\prime}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}
refines
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}
, if
P^{\prime}\preceq P
.
We let
\bot
refine all the configurations, and define distribution refinement by linearity.

A process is expected to be capable of matching any move of its refinements, thus, when considering
P=M_{01}(q\rhd{}x).(Q+Q^{\prime})
, the moves of all
P^{\prime}\preceq P
should be available for
P
, included the ones of
M_{01}(q\rhd{}x).\textbf{if }x=0\textbf{ then }Q\textbf{ else }Q^{\prime}
where the choice between
Q
and
Q^{\prime}
depends on the outcome of the measurement.

We prove in the following that our constraints on non-determinism are not too restrictive, namely, that a distribution can simulate all its refinements.

Theorem 4.12.

Let
\Delta^{\prime}\preceq\Delta
. If
\Delta^{\prime}\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Theta^{\prime}
then
\Delta\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Theta
for some
\Theta
such that
\Theta^{\prime}\preceq\Theta
.

Proof sketch.

We prove by rule induction that
whenever
P^{\prime}\preceq P
and
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P^{\prime},R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta^{\prime}
then
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta
for some
\Delta
such that
\Delta^{\prime}\preceq\Delta
.
The proof for
Congr
relies on the fact that refinement and structural congruence works well together.
In detail, we show that given
P^{\prime}\preceq P
with
P^{\prime}\equiv Q^{\prime}
we can find some
Q
such that
Q^{\prime}\preceq Q
and
P\equiv Q
.
In the other cases it suffices to use the induction hypothesis.
The theorem then holds by decomposability of
\longsquiggly_{\pi}
.
∎

As a result of this property, the distributions
\Delta
and
\Theta
of
4.10
are distinguishable.

Example 4.13.

Consider
\Delta
and
\Theta
of
4.10
, and notice
that
\Delta^{\prime}\preceq\Delta
, where
\Delta^{\prime}
sends on
c
if and only if the qubit is in
\ket{0}
.
Formally
\Delta^{\prime}=\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{+}{+},M_{01}(q\rhd{}x).\textbf{if }x=0\textbf{ then }c!q\textbf{ else }d!q,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
(see
Figure 8
).
By performing this choice,
\Delta^{\prime}
is implicitly communicating the outcome of the measurement to the observer (through a side-channel, we could say).
Consider the context

\displaystyle O[{\,\cdot\,}]=[{\,\cdot\,}]\parallel(c?x.M_{01}(x\rhd{}y).(\textbf{if }y=0\textbf{ then }z!0\textbf{ else }o!0\parallel\mathbf{0}_{x}))+(d?x.\tau.\mathbf{0}_{x})

and note that, after two steps,
O[\Delta^{\prime}]
reduces to a distribution expressing barb
z
but not
o
, which is impossible for
O[\Theta]
.
As a result of
Theorem 4.12
,
\Delta
can replicate this move of
\Delta^{\prime}
, hence
\Delta\not\sim_{cs}\Theta
.

Our constrained bisimilarity is the first one to verify both
Theorem 4.8
and
4.12
,
while all the previously proposed ones either fail in equating indistinguishable quantum states, or overly constrain non-determinism
(see
Table 1
for an in-depth comparison).

\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{+}{+},M_{01}(q\rhd{}x).(c!q+d!q),\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}

4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-8.15433pt{\frac{1}{2}}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{1}{1},d!q,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
\preceq
=
\scriptstyle\diamond
\scriptstyle\diamond

Figure 8.

The existence of the dashed arrow on the right is guaranteed by the solid one on the left by
Theorem 4.12
.

4.4.
Properties of Constrained Bisimilarity

We now investigate our cs-bisimilarity.
We first recover two defining properties of
(
Deng and Feng 2012
)
,
namely that
\sim_{cs}
is closed for superoperator application on qubits not appearing in the processes,
and that the state of such qubits is required to match in bisimilar distributions.
Then we show that discarded qubits can be “traced out” from the quantum state without affecting bisimilarity.
Finally, we discuss up-to techniques for proving constrained bisimilarity.

We start by recovering trace-identity and closure over superoperator application.

Proposition 4.14.

Let
\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\sim_{cs}\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\sigma,Q,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
,
then

(1)

\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{q}}\!\left(\rho\right)\!,P,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\sim_{cs}\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{q}}\!\left(\sigma\right)\!,Q,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
, for any
\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{q}}
and
\tilde{q}
not in
\tilde{\Sigma}_{P}
;

(2)

\tr_{\Sigma_{P}}(\rho)=\tr_{\Sigma_{P}}(\sigma)
.

Proof sketch.

For the first point, take any superoperator
\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{q}}
, then there is a context that performs such transformation, so a context-closed relation is necessarily superoperator-closed.
For the second one, we proceed by contradiction: if the reduced density operators of two configurations are different, then we can build a context which measures such qubits obtaining a different distribution of outcomes and thus distinguishing the configurations via fresh barbs.
∎

Note that this is a useful result for disproving bisimilarity, as e.g., distributions with different partial trace are immediately deemed distinguishable.

A result that helps instead in proving bisimilarity is that the state of discarded qubits can be ignored.
In order to prove this in general, we extend the partial trace as follows.

Definition 4.15.

The partial trace
\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\right)
over
\tilde{q}
of a configuration
is defined as
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\rho\right),P^{\prime},R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}
if
P\equiv P^{\prime}\parallel\mathbf{0}_{\tilde{q}}
.
We let
\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\bot\right)=\bot
, and define the partial trace of distributions by linearity.

Intuitively, we remove the discarded qubits
together with
the discard processes.
Note that such an operation is defined only on distributions of configurations that discard the same qubits.

Proposition 4.16.

Let
\Delta,\Theta
be distributions such that
\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\Delta\right)
and
\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\Theta\right)
are well-defined for a given
\tilde{q}
. If
\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\Delta\right)\sim_{cs}\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\Theta\right)
then
\Delta\sim_{cs}\Theta
.

Proof sketch.

We show, by induction on
\longsquiggly_{\pi}
, that the semantics of
\mathcal{C}
and of
\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\mathcal{C}\right)
are equivalent, and since the partial trace does not affect barbs, the desired property follows.
∎

Note that, even if this property is given for the process
\mathbf{0}_{\tilde{q}}
, we can apply it to any “discard-like” process thanks to the linearity of
\sim_{cs}
, i.e. to any process bisimilar to
\mathbf{0}_{\tilde{q}}
.

The capacity to ignore discarded qubit is useful in a lot of proofs, as for the example below.

Example 4.17.

The two distributions below are bisimilar

\displaystyle\Delta=\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{\Phi^+}{\Phi^+},M_{01}(q_{1}\rhd{}x).c!q_{1}\parallel\mathbf{0}_{q_{2}},\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\text{ and }\Theta=\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{\Phi^+}{\Phi^+},M_{\pm}(q_{1}\rhd{}x).c!q_{1}\parallel\mathbf{0}_{q_{2}},\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}

Taken any
O[{\,\cdot\,}]
, they evolve in
O[\Delta^{\prime}]
with

\Delta^{\prime}=\Big(\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{00}{00},c!q_{1},\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-31.70206pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern and
O[\Theta^{\prime}]
with

\Theta^{\prime}=\Big(\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{++}{++},c!q_{1},\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-31.70206pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern .

Finally, note that
\tr_{q_{2}}(\Delta^{\prime})\sim_{cs}\tr_{q_{2}}(\Theta^{\prime})
holds because
\tr_{q_{2}}(\Delta^{\prime})=\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\outerproduct{0}{0},c!q_{1},\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{1}/{2}}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-31.70206pt\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.34999pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-15.5321pt{{1}/{2}}\kern
