and

\displaystyle{\llparenthesis\,}\mathcal{C}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}

\displaystyle=\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,((c!v+P_{1})\parallel P_{2}\setminus D)\parallel(c?x.{\llparenthesis\,}R_{1}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k\iota}+k!0+{\llparenthesis\,}R_{2}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k})}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,((c!v+P_{1})\parallel P_{2}\parallel(c?x.{\llparenthesis\,}R_{1}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k\iota}+k!0+{\llparenthesis\,}R_{2}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}))\setminus D}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}

\displaystyle\to\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,(P_{2}\parallel{\llparenthesis\,}R_{1}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k\iota}[\nicefrac{{v}}{{x}}])\setminus D}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}

\displaystyle\equiv\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,(P_{2}\setminus D)\parallel{\llparenthesis\,}R_{1}[\nicefrac{{v}}{{x}}]{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k\iota}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}={\llparenthesis\,}\Delta{\,\rrparenthesis}^{\varepsilon}_{k}

{\llparenthesis\,}\mathcal{C}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}\to{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta{\,\rrparenthesis}^{\varepsilon}_{k}:k
holds trivially as for the
OQOp
.

If rule
Output
is valid, then
R\equiv c!v
, we have

\mathcal{C}=\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,(c?x.P_{1})+P_{2})\parallel P_{3}\setminus D,c!v}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\longsquiggly_{\varepsilon}\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,(P_{1}[\nicefrac{{v}}{{x}}]\parallel P_{3})\setminus D,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}=\Delta

and

\displaystyle{\llparenthesis\,}\mathcal{C}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}

\displaystyle=\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,(((c?x.P_{1})+P_{2})\parallel P_{3}\setminus D)\parallel(c!v+k!0)}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,(((c?x.P_{1})+P_{2})\parallel P_{3}\parallel(c!v+k!0))\setminus D}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}

\displaystyle\to\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,(P_{1}[\nicefrac{{v}}{{x}}]\parallel P_{3}\parallel\mathbf{0})\setminus D}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}

\displaystyle\equiv\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,(P_{1}[\nicefrac{{v}}{{x}}]\parallel P_{3}\setminus D)\parallel\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}={\llparenthesis\,}\Delta{\,\rrparenthesis}^{\varepsilon}_{k}

{\llparenthesis\,}\mathcal{C}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}\to{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta{\,\rrparenthesis}^{\varepsilon}_{k}:k
holds trivially as for the
QInput
.

If rule
ParL
is valid, then
R\equiv R_{1}\parallel R_{2}
, we have

\mathcal{C}=\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,R_{1}\parallel R_{2}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\longsquiggly_{\ell\lambda}\Theta\parallel R_{2}=\Delta

By inductive hypothesis, for any
k

\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,R_{1}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\longsquiggly_{\lambda}\Theta\Longrightarrow\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P\parallel{\llparenthesis\,}R_{1}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\to{\llparenthesis\,}\Theta{\,\rrparenthesis}^{\lambda}_{k}:k\lambda

and

\displaystyle{\llparenthesis\,}\mathcal{C}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}=\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P\parallel{\llparenthesis\,}R_{1}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k\ell}\parallel{\llparenthesis\,}R_{2}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{kr}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\to{\llparenthesis\,}\Theta{\,\rrparenthesis}^{\lambda}_{k\ell}\parallel{\llparenthesis\,}R_{2}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{kr}={\llparenthesis\,}\Delta{\,\rrparenthesis}^{\ell\lambda}_{k}

{\llparenthesis\,}\mathcal{C}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}\to{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta{\,\rrparenthesis}^{\ell\lambda}_{k}:k\ell\lambda
holds by inductive hypothesis by setting
k=k\ell
.
The case for
ParR
is analogous to the case for
ParL
.

Finally, the
Congr
rule is simply resolved by induction, since its trivial to show that the congruence relation does not change the barbs expressed by a distribution.
∎

Lemma C.5.

For any observer
R
and index
\lambda\neq\diamond
, we have

\forall k\ \left(\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P\parallel{\llparenthesis\,}R{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Delta:k\lambda\Longrightarrow\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\longsquiggly_{\lambda}\Delta^{\prime}\text{ with }{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta^{\prime}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}^{\lambda}\equiv\Delta\right)

Proof.

Note that any transition
{\llparenthesis\,}\mathcal{C}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}=\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P\parallel{\llparenthesis\,}R{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Delta:k\lambda
must involve
R
, because a transition modifying only
P
would not be tagged as
k\lambda
. We will then proceed on induction on the syntax of
R
.

If
R=\mathbf{0}_{\tilde{e}}
, there are no
k\lambda
-tagged transitions going out from
{\llparenthesis\,}\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P\parallel\mathbf{0}_{\tilde{e}}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}{\,\rrparenthesis}=\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P\parallel\mathbf{0}_{\tilde{e}}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}
.

If
R=\mathcal{E}\!\left(\tilde{x}\right)\!.R^{\prime}
, we have

{\llparenthesis\,}\mathcal{C}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}\equiv\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P\parallel\mathcal{E}\!\left(\tilde{x}\right)\!.{\llparenthesis\,}R^{\prime}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k\iota}+k!0}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Delta\equiv\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{e}}\!\left(\rho\right)\!,P\parallel{\llparenthesis\,}R^{\prime}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k\iota}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}~:~k

But then

\displaystyle\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,\mathcal{E}\!\left(\tilde{x}\right)\!.R^{\prime}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\longsquiggly_{\varepsilon}\Delta^{\prime}=\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{e}}\!\left(\rho\right)\!,P,R^{\prime}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}},\text{ and }

\displaystyle{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta^{\prime}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}^{\varepsilon}=\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{e}}\!\left(\rho\right)\!,P\parallel{\llparenthesis\,}R^{\prime}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}^{\varepsilon}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}=\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{e}}\!\left(\rho\right)\!,P\parallel{\llparenthesis\,}R^{\prime}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k\iota}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\equiv\Delta.

If
R=M(\tilde{x}\rhd{}y).R^{\prime}
, we have

\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P\parallel M(\tilde{x}\rhd{}y).{\llparenthesis\,}R^{\prime}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k\iota}+\varepsilon!0}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Delta\equiv\sum_{m}p_{m}\bullet\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho_{m},P\parallel{\llparenthesis\,}R^{\prime}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k\iota}[m/y]}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}:\varepsilon

for some set of
p_{m}
and
\rho_{m}
. Then the proof is identical to the previous case, noticing that
{\llparenthesis\,}R[v/x]{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}^{\lambda}={\llparenthesis\,}R{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}^{\lambda}[v/x]
as the tags depends on the position of the processes and not on their content.

If
R=c!e
, then
P
must be receiving on the unrestricted channel
c
, so we have

{\llparenthesis\,}\mathcal{C}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}\equiv\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,(((c?x.P^{\prime})+P^{\prime\prime})\parallel Q\parallel(c!v+\varepsilon!0))\setminus D}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Delta\equiv\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,(P^{\prime}[v/x]\parallel Q)\setminus D}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}~:~\varepsilon

for some (possibly empty) set of restrictions
D
and processes
P^{\prime},P^{\prime\prime},Q
. But then

\mathcal{C}\equiv_{o}\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,(((c?x.P^{\prime})+P^{\prime\prime})\parallel Q)\setminus D,c!v}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\longsquiggly_{\varepsilon}\Delta^{\prime}=\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,(P^{\prime}[v/x]\parallel Q)\setminus D,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
and
{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta^{\prime}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}^{\varepsilon}\equiv\Delta
.

If
R
is a sum of reception, then
P
must be sending on at least one of the channel in
R
, so we have

{\llparenthesis\,}\mathcal{C}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}\equiv\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,(((c!v+P)\parallel Q\parallel(c?x.{\llparenthesis\,}R^{\prime}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k\iota}+{\llparenthesis\,}T{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}+\varepsilon!0))\setminus D}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Delta\equiv\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,(Q\setminus D)\parallel R^{\prime}[v/x]}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}~:~\varepsilon

for some (possibly empty) set of restrictions
D
and process
Q
, and the proof is identical to the previous case.

If
R=\textbf{if }e\textbf{ then }R_{1}\textbf{ else }R_{2}
, then the only way to have a transition is after applying the structural congruence and obtaining either
R_{1}
or
R_{2}
, from which the desired property is obtained thanks to the inductive hypothesis and the fact that
{\llparenthesis\,}R{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}\equiv{\llparenthesis\,}R^{\prime}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}
if and only if
R\equiv_{o}R^{\prime}
.

If
R=R_{1}\parallel R_{2}
and
{\llparenthesis\,}\mathcal{C}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}\equiv\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P\parallel{\llparenthesis\,}R_{1}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{kl}\parallel{\llparenthesis\,}R_{2}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{kr}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Delta:k\lambda
, then the transition cannot be a synchronisation between
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
because all the tags of
{\llparenthesis\,}C{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}
must be in
\Delta
except from
k\lambda
, and a synchronisation would destroy two tags, not just one. Suppose then that the transition involves
R_{1}
, the
R_{2}
case is symmetric. From the
SemPar
rule must be
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P\parallel{\llparenthesis\,}R_{1}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k\ell}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Delta_{\ell}
with
\Delta=\Delta_{\ell}\parallel{\llparenthesis\,}R_{2}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{kr}
. This transition is still tagged with
k\lambda
, as the
k\lambda
tag is present in
{\llparenthesis\,}R_{1}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k\ell}\parallel{\llparenthesis\,}R_{2}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{kr}
but is absent in
\Delta_{\ell}\parallel{\llparenthesis\,}R_{2}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{kr}
, no must be in
{\llparenthesis\,}R_{1}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{kl}
. Besides, we know that
k\lambda=k\ell\lambda^{\prime}
for some
\lambda^{\prime}
, as it is easy to verify that all the tags in
{\llparenthesis\,}R_{1}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k\ell}
start with
k\ell
. Then we can apply the inductive hypothesis

\displaystyle\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P\parallel{\llparenthesis\,}R_{1}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k\ell}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Delta_{\ell}:k\ell\lambda^{\prime}\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,R_{1}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\longsquiggly_{\lambda^{\prime}}\Delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\text{ with }{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta_{\ell}^{\prime}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k\ell}^{\lambda^{\prime}}=\Delta_{\ell}

and derive

\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,R_{1}\parallel R_{2}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\longsquiggly_{\lambda}\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\parallel R_{2}\text{ with }{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta^{\prime}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}^{\lambda}=\Delta

since
\lambda=\ell\lambda^{\prime}
and
{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta^{\prime}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}^{\lambda}={\llparenthesis\,}\Delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\parallel R_{2}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}^{\ell\lambda^{\prime}}={\llparenthesis\,}\Delta_{\ell}^{\prime}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k\ell}^{\lambda^{\prime}}\parallel{\llparenthesis\,}R_{2}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{kr}=\Delta_{\ell}\parallel{\llparenthesis\,}R_{2}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{kr}=\Delta
.
∎

Lemma C.6.

For any
\Delta,\Delta^{\prime}
that do not contain
\bot
, we have

\forall k\ \left(\Delta\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta^{\prime}\Longleftrightarrow{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta{\,\rrparenthesis}\rightarrow\Delta^{\prime\prime}:\pi\text{ with }{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta^{\prime}{\,\rrparenthesis}_{k}\equiv\Delta^{\prime\prime}\right)

Proof.

The proof follows from
C.3
,
C.4
and
C.5
, thanks to linearity of
\longsquiggly_{\pi}
and
\rightarrow~:~\pi

∎

We can now finally prove
Theorem 4.5

See
4.5

Proof.

The direction
\sim_{cs}\not\subseteq\sim_{s}
is given by
4.6
.
For
\sim_{s}\subseteq\sim_{cs}
, we define the relation
\mathcal{R}=\{(\Delta,\Theta)\mid{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta{\,\rrparenthesis}\sim_{s}{\llparenthesis\,}\Theta{\,\rrparenthesis}\}

And then we prove that
\mathcal{R}
is a constrained distribution bisimulation. Notice that, taken
\Delta,\Theta\in\sim_{s}
, when we interpret them as distribution of triples they have a
\mathbf{0}
observer, and so
{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta{\,\rrparenthesis}=\Delta
and
{\llparenthesis\,}\Theta{\,\rrparenthesis}=\Theta
. This means that
\sim_{s}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
, and if
\mathcal{R}
is a bisimulation then
\sim_{s}\subseteq\sim_{cs}
.
Notice also that
\mathcal{R}
is context-closed, meaning that if
\Delta,\Theta\in\mathcal{R}
, then also
O[\Delta],O[\Theta]\in\mathcal{R}
for any
O[{\,\cdot\,}]
, because
\sim_{s}
is context-closed. Finally,
\mathcal{R}
is linear and decomposable, as
{\llparenthesis\,}{\,\cdot\,}{\,\rrparenthesis}
is defined by linearity and
\sim_{s}
is linear and decomposable (as proven in
(
Hennessy 2012
)
, proposition 5.8).

Take
\Delta,\Theta\in\mathcal{R}
. Since
{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta{\,\rrparenthesis}
and
{\llparenthesis\,}\Theta{\,\rrparenthesis}
are bisimilar, they express the same barbs. But
{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta{\,\rrparenthesis}
expresses at least all the barbs of
\Delta
(and similarly
{\llparenthesis\,}\Theta{\,\rrparenthesis}
), so also
\Delta
and
\Theta
must express the same barbs.

Suppose
\Delta\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta^{\prime}
, we will prove that there exists a transition
\Theta\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Theta^{\prime}
with
\Delta^{\prime}\mathcal{R}\Theta^{\prime}
, the other direction is symmetric. Let
\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta^{\prime}_{\not\bot}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\overline{\bot}
, where
\Delta^{\prime}_{\not\bot}
does not contain
\bot
. Then, by decomposability of
\longsquiggly_{\pi}
and of
\mathcal{R}
, it must be
\Delta=\Delta_{\not\bot}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\Delta_{\bot}
, and also
\Theta=\Theta_{\not\bot}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\Theta\bot
. Since
\Delta_{\bot}\longsquiggly_{\pi}\bot
, from
C.6
it follows that there is no
\Delta^{\prime}
such that
{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta_{\bot}{\,\rrparenthesis}\rightarrow\Delta^{\prime}~:~\pi
. But since
{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta_{\bot}{\,\rrparenthesis}\sim_{s}{\llparenthesis\,}\Theta_{\bot}{\,\rrparenthesis}
, using the third property of bisimilarity (from
\Theta
to
\Delta
) we get that there is no
\Theta^{\prime}
either such that
{\llparenthesis\,}\Theta_{\bot}{\,\rrparenthesis}\rightarrow\Theta^{\prime}~:~\pi
. Then, from
C.6
it follows that
\Theta_{\bot}\longsquiggly_{\pi}\bot
, and of course
(\bot,\bot)\in\mathcal{R}
.

Consider now
\Delta_{\not\bot}
. From
\Delta_{\not\bot}\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta^{\prime}_{\not\bot}
, we know that
{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta_{\not\bot}{\,\rrparenthesis}\rightarrow{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta^{\prime}_{\not\bot}{\,\rrparenthesis}^{\pi}~:~\pi
tanks to
C.6
, and so

{\llparenthesis\,}\Theta_{\not\bot}{\,\rrparenthesis}\rightarrow\Theta^{\prime}
for a
\Theta^{\prime}\sim_{s}{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta^{\prime}_{\not\bot}{\,\rrparenthesis}^{\pi}
. But since they are bisimilar, they express the same barbs, and from
{\llparenthesis\,}\Theta_{\not\bot}{\,\rrparenthesis}\rightarrow\Theta^{\prime}~:~\pi
we
get
\Theta_{\not\bot}\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Theta^{\prime\prime}
with
{\llparenthesis\,}\Theta^{\prime\prime}{\,\rrparenthesis}^{\pi}\equiv\Theta^{\prime}
.
To sum up, we have

\begin{matrix}\Delta=\Delta_{\not\bot}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\Delta_{\bot}&\mathcal{R}&\Theta=\Theta_{\not\bot}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern \rotatebox[origin]{270.0}{$\longsquiggly$}_{\pi}&&\rotatebox[origin]{270.0}{$\longsquiggly$}_{\pi}\\
\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta^{\prime}_{\not\bot}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\bot&?&\Theta^{\prime}=\Theta^{\prime\prime}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\bot\end{matrix}

with
{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta^{\prime}_{\not\bot}{\,\rrparenthesis}^{\pi}\sim_{s}{\llparenthesis\,}\Theta^{\prime\prime}{\,\rrparenthesis}^{\pi}
. It’s possible to verify that
{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta{\,\rrparenthesis}^{\pi}\sim_{s}{\llparenthesis\,}\Theta{\,\rrparenthesis}^{\pi}
implies
{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta{\,\rrparenthesis}\sim_{s}{\llparenthesis\,}\Theta{\,\rrparenthesis}
, as there is a bijection
f
between the free channels of
{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta{\,\rrparenthesis}
and of
{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta{\,\rrparenthesis}^{\pi}
, and so any context
O[{\,\cdot\,}]
applied on
{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta{\,\rrparenthesis}
has the same behaviour of the context
f(O[{\,\cdot\,}])
applied on
{\llparenthesis\,}\Delta{\,\rrparenthesis}^{\pi}
, and vice versa. Given that, since
\bot,\bot\in\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{R}
is linear, we can conclude
\Delta^{\prime}\mathcal{R}\Theta^{\prime}
.
∎

C.2.
Proof of
Theorem 4.4
and
Theorem 4.8

Before proving
Theorem 4.4
and
Theorem 4.8
, it is convenient to prove the soundness of the bisimilarity up to convex hull technique
(
Bonchi et al. 2017
)
.
To do so, let us define the function on relations
b
, which is the function of which bisimilarity is the greatest fix point

b(\mathcal{R})\coloneqq\left\{(\Delta,\Theta)\biggm|\begin{array}[]{c}\Delta\downarrow_{b}^{p}\Leftrightarrow\Theta\downarrow_{b}^{p}\\
O[\Delta]\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta^{\prime}\Rightarrow\exists\Theta^{\prime}\ O[\Theta]\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Theta^{\prime}\wedge\Delta^{\prime}\,\mathcal{R}\,\Theta^{\prime}\\
O[\Theta]\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Theta^{\prime}\Rightarrow\exists\Delta^{\prime}\ O[\Delta]\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta^{\prime}\wedge\Delta^{\prime}\,\mathcal{R}\,\Theta^{\prime}\end{array}\right\}

and recall the definition of convex hull from
(
Bonchi et al. 2017
)

Cv(\mathcal{R})\coloneqq\bigg\{\bigg(\sum_{i\in I}p_{i}\bullet\Delta_{i},\sum_{i\in I}p_{i}\bullet\Theta_{i}\bigg)\ \bigg|\ \forall i\in I\ldotp\Delta_{i}\,\mathcal{R}\,\Theta_{i}\bigg\}

Observe that both
b
and
Cv
are monotone functions on the lattice of relations.

Lemma C.7 (
Cv
is
b
-compatible).

We have that
\forall\mathcal{R}\ldotp Cv(b(\mathcal{R}))\subseteq b(Cv(\mathcal{R}))
.

Proof.

Assume
(\Delta,\Theta)\in Cv(b(\mathcal{R}))
. Then it must be
\Delta=\sum_{i\in I}p_{i}\bullet\Delta_{i}
and
\Theta=\sum_{i\in I}p_{i}\bullet\Theta_{i}
for a certain set of probabilities
\{p_{i}\}_{i\in I}
, with

\Delta_{i}\,b(\mathcal{R})\,\Theta_{i}
. So we have, for any
i\in I

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathcal{C}\downarrow_{c}}\Delta_{i}(\mathcal{C})=\sum_{\mathcal{C}\downarrow_{c}}\Theta_{i}(\mathcal{C})=q_{i}

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathcal{C}\downarrow_{c}}\sum_{i\in I}p_{i}\Delta_{i}(\mathcal{C})=\sum_{\mathcal{C}\downarrow_{c}}\sum_{i\in I}p_{i}\Theta_{i}(\mathcal{C})=\sum_{i\in I}p_{i}q_{i}=q

meaning that
\Delta\downarrow_{c}^{q}
if and only if
\Theta\downarrow_{c}^{q}
. The same holds also for
\Delta\downarrow_{\bot}^{q}
and
\Theta\downarrow_{\bot}^{q}
, as

\displaystyle\Delta_{i}(\bot)=\Theta_{i}(\bot)=q_{i}

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I}p_{i}\Delta_{i}(\bot)=\sum_{i\in I}p_{i}\Theta_{i}(\bot)=\sum_{i\in I}p_{i}q_{i}=q

Suppose
O[\Delta]=\sum_{i\in I}p_{i}\bullet O[\Delta_{i}]\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta^{\prime}
.
From
(
Hennessy 2012
)
,
\longsquiggly_{\pi}
is left-decomposable, so it must be
\Delta^{\prime}=\sum_{i\in I}p_{i}\bullet\Delta_{i}^{\prime}
with
O[\Delta_{i}]\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta_{i}^{\prime}
. But
since
\Delta_{i}\,b(\mathcal{R})\,\Theta_{i}
it must be
O[\Theta_{i}]\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Theta_{i}^{\prime}
with
\Delta_{i}^{\prime}\,\mathcal{R}\,\Theta_{i}^{\prime}
for any
i\in I
, from which it follows that

\sum_{i\in I}p_{i}\bullet O[\Theta_{i}]\longsquiggly_{\pi}\sum_{i\in I}p_{i}\bullet\Theta_{i}^{\prime}=\Theta^{\prime}
.

In other words,
\Delta
and
\Theta
express the same barbs and whenever

O[\Delta]\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta^{\prime}
, there exists a transition
O[\Theta]\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Theta^{\prime}
such that
\Delta^{\prime}\,Cv(\mathcal{R})\,\Theta^{\prime}
(the symmetrical
argument is the same). So we can conclude that
(\Delta,\Theta)\in b(Cv(\mathcal{R}))
.
∎

From
(
Pous and Sangiorgi 2011
)
, we know that if
Cv
is compatible, then bisimulations up to
Cv
are a sound proof technique.
Besides, it also allows us to show linearity of
\sim_{cs}
as a corollary (note that the same proof holds also for
\sim_{s}
).

See
4.4

Proof.

We can easily prove that
Cv(\sim_{cs})\subseteq\ \sim_{cs}
. Since
Cv
is

b
-compatible, we have that
Cv(b(\sim_{cs}))\subseteq b(Cv(\sim_{cs}))
, and
since
\sim_{cs}
is the greatest fix point of
b
, we have
Cv(\sim_{cs})\subseteq b(Cv(\sim_{cs}))
, meaning that
Cv(\sim_{cs})
is a bisimulation, and so

Cv(\sim_{cs})\subseteq\ \sim_{cs}
.
∎

In order to prove
Theorem 4.8
, we first need a lemma on deterministic distributions.

Lemma C.8.

If
\Delta,\Theta
are deterministic, then
\Delta\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\Theta
is deterministic for each probability

Proof.

We will prove that, for any probability

\mathcal{A}=\left\{\Delta\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\Theta\mid\Delta,\Theta\text{ are deterministic}\right\}

is a deterministic set.
If
(\Delta\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\Theta)\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Xi^{\prime}
, and

, since

is decomposable it must be
\Xi^{\prime}=\Delta^{\prime}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\Theta^{\prime}
and
\Xi^{\prime\prime}=\Delta^{\prime\prime}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\Theta^{\prime\prime}
, with
\Delta\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta^{\prime}
,
\Delta\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
,
\Theta\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Theta^{\prime}
and
\Theta\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Theta^{\prime\prime}
. But then, since
\Delta
and
\Theta
are deterministic, we have
\Delta^{\prime}\sim_{cs}\Delta^{\prime\prime}
,
\Theta^{\prime}\sim_{cs}\Theta^{\prime\prime}
and they are all deterministic, so
\Xi^{\prime},\Xi^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{A}
and
\Xi^{\prime}\sim_{cs}\Xi^{\prime\prime}
, for linearity of
\sim_{cs}
.
∎

See
4.8

Proof.

For any deterministic
P
, we define

\mathcal{R}^{\prime}=\left\{\left(\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern \overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern and prove that
\mathcal{R}=\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\cup\{(\bot,\bot)\}
is a bisimulation up to
Cv
and up to bisimilarity. That is, we require that if
\Delta\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta^{\prime}
then
\Theta\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Theta^{\prime}
with
\Delta^{\prime}\sim_{cs}Cv(\mathcal{R})\sim_{cs}\Theta^{\prime}
.
Since
Cv
is compatible, bisimulation up to
Cv
and up to bisimilarity is a valid proof technique
(
Pous and Sangiorgi 2011
)
.

The case
\Delta=\Theta=\bot
is straightforward. Otherwise, let

, with

. Since they have the same process and observer, they are typed by the same context
\Sigma
. Notice that we do not need to quantify over any context
O[{\,\cdot\,}]
, because
\mathcal{R}
is a saturated relation, meaning that if
\Delta,\Theta\in\mathcal{R}
, then
O[\Delta],O[\Theta]\in\mathcal{R}
for any context.

For the first condition, we have that
\Delta\downarrow_{c}^{p}
if and only if
p=1
and
P
or
R
express the barb
c
. Then, also
\Theta
expresses the barb
c
with probability one, and vice versa.

For the second condition, suppose that
\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\nu,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta^{\prime}
. We will proceed by induction on
\longsquiggly_{\pi}
to prove that
\Theta\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Theta^{\prime}
with
\Delta^{\prime}Cv(\mathcal{R})\Theta^{\prime}
. The “classical” cases, that do not modify the quantum state, are trivial, since if
\Delta\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta^{\prime}
then
\Theta
can go in
\Theta^{\prime}
performing the same move in both configurations, and
\Delta^{\prime}\mathcal{R}\Theta^{\prime}
. The only interesting cases are
QOp
and
QMeas
for the process, and
OQOp
and
OQMeas
for the observer.

In the

case, if

\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\nu,\mathcal{E}\!\left(\tilde{x}\right)\!.P^{\prime},R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\longsquiggly_{\diamond}\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{x}}\!\left(\nu\right)\!,P^{\prime},R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}

then

4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\sigma\right)\!=\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{x}}\!\left(\nu\right)\!
, thanks to linearity of superoperators. The
OQOp
case is identical.

In the
QMeas
case, we have

\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\nu,M(\tilde{x}\rhd{}y).P^{\prime},R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\longsquiggly_{\diamond}\Delta^{\prime}

with

\Delta^{\prime}=\sum_{m}tr_{m}(\nu)\bullet\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\nu^{\prime}_{m},P^{\prime}[\nicefrac{{m}}{{y}}],R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\qquad\nu^{\prime}_{m}=\frac{\mathcal{M}_{m}\!\left(\nu\right)\!}{tr_{m}(\nu)}

where
\mathcal{M}_{m}\!\left(\nu\right)\!=(M_{m}\otimes I)\nu(M_{m}\otimes I)^{\dagger}
is the trace-nonincreasing superoperator corresponding to outcome
m
when measuring qubits
\tilde{x}
, and
tr_{m}{\nu}=tr(\mathcal{M}_{m}\!\left(\nu\right)\!)
is the probability of said outcome.
Then

8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern with

\displaystyle\Theta^{\prime}_{\rho}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m}tr_{m}(\rho)\bullet\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho^{\prime}_{m},P^{\prime}[\nicefrac{{m}}{{y}}],R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\qquad\rho^{\prime}_{m}=\frac{\mathcal{M}_{m}\!\left(\rho\right)\!}{tr_{m}(\rho)}

is equal to

, thanks to linearity of superoperators and trace. So, according to the rules of probability distributions,
\Theta^{\prime}_{\rho}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\Theta^{\prime}_{\sigma}
can be rewritten as

with

. It’s easy to show that

\Delta^{\prime}\ Cv(\mathcal{R})\ \left(\Theta^{\prime}_{\rho}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-16.0711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt}}_{{\kern-10.64743pt{p}\kern 8.071pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-11.3782pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.6321pt}}_{{\kern-7.50415pt{p}\kern 5.6321pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{p}}}}\oplus^{{\kern-10.28932pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt}}_{{\kern-6.41527pt{p}\kern 4.54321pt}}}\Theta^{\prime}_{\sigma}\right)
.

The

and
\longsquiggly_{\pi}

For the third condition, we must prove that if

for some

, so we have that either

or

with

. In the first case, also

For the second case, we say

if there exists a
\Theta^{\prime}
such that

and
\bot\not\in\lceil\Theta^{\prime}\rceil
. We will show that, if

.

and the transition

with

P=P\setminus c
or

, the required property follows trivially from the inductive hypothesis.

Suppose that

, we proceed by induction on

. If

or

and it must be

If

, the case is symmetrical.

If

, the required property follows trivially from the inductive hypothesis.
∎

Consider now the refinement relation
\preceq
, defined as
P^{\prime}\preceq P
if
P^{\prime}
can be obtained from
P
by substituting some occurrence of
Q+Q^{\prime}
with either
Q
,
Q^{\prime}
or
\textbf{if }e\textbf{ then }Q\textbf{ else }Q^{\prime}
for some
e
.
We extend the relation to configurations writing
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho^{\prime},P^{\prime}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\preceq\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}
and
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho^{\prime},P^{\prime},R^{\prime}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\preceq\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}
when
\rho^{\prime}=\rho
,
P^{\prime}\preceq P
, and
R^{\prime}=R
.
Moreover, we lift
\preceq
to distributions by linearity and imposing
\bot\preceq\Delta
for any
\Delta
.

C.3.
Proof of
Theorem 4.12

To prove
Theorem 4.12
we start from some auxiliary lemmas.

Lemma C.9.

Let
P^{\prime}\preceq P
, if
P^{\prime}\equiv Q^{\prime}
then
P\equiv Q
for some
Q
such that
Q^{\prime}\preceq Q
.

Proof.

Trivial by cases on the rules for
\equiv
.
∎

Lemma C.10.

Let
P^{\prime}\preceq P
, if
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P^{\prime}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Delta^{\prime}
then
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Delta
for some
\Delta
such that
\Delta^{\prime}\preceq\Delta
.

Proof.

Consider the case
\Delta^{\prime}\neq\bot
.
We proceed by induction on the rules for
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P^{\prime}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Delta^{\prime}
.

If the rule is
Tau
, then
P^{\prime}=\tau.Q_{1}^{\prime}+Q_{2}^{\prime}
, with
\Delta^{\prime}=\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,Q_{1}^{\prime}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
and can only be the refinement of
P=\tau.Q_{1}+Q_{2}
for
Q_{i}^{\prime}\preceq Q_{i}
.
Then
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,Q_{1}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
, and the refinement holds by definition.

The same applies to
Restrict
,
QOP
and
QMeas
by noticing that
P^{\prime}\setminus c\preceq P
only if
P=P^{\prime\prime}\setminus c
, and that
P^{\prime}\preceq P
implies
P^{\prime}[v/x]\preceq P[v/x]
for any
v
and
x
.

Consider
Par
, then
P^{\prime}=Q_{1}^{\prime}\parallel Q_{2}^{\prime}
, with
\Delta^{\prime}=\Theta^{\prime}\parallel Q_{2}^{\prime}
for some
\Theta^{\prime}
such that
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,Q_{1}^{\prime}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Theta^{\prime}
.
Notice that
P
must be equal
Q_{1}\parallel Q_{2}
for some
Q_{1}
and
Q_{2}
such that
Q_{i}^{\prime}\preceq Q_{i}
.
By induction hypothesis,
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,Q_{1}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Theta
for some
\Theta
such that
\Theta^{\prime}\preceq\Theta
, and thus, by
Par
rule,
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,Q_{1}\parallel Q_{2}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Theta\parallel Q_{2}
, and
\Theta^{\prime}\parallel Q_{2}^{\prime}\preceq\Theta\parallel Q_{2}
by definition.

Consider
Congr
and assume
P^{\prime}\equiv Q^{\prime}
,
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,Q^{\prime}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Theta^{\prime}
and
\Theta^{\prime}\equiv\Delta^{\prime}
.
By
C.9
, there exists some
Q
such that
P\equiv Q
and
Q^{\prime}\preceq Q
.
Then, by induction hypothesis,
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,Q}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Theta
with
\Theta^{\prime}\preceq\Theta
.
From
\Theta^{\prime}\equiv\Delta^{\prime}
and
\Theta^{\prime}\preceq\Theta
, we know by
C.9
that
\Theta\equiv\Delta
for some
\Delta
such that
\Delta^{\prime}\preceq\Delta
.
Then
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Delta
by applying
Congr
.

Finally, consider the case in which
\Delta^{\prime}=\bot
.
Then,
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Delta
either for
\Delta=\bot
or for some
\Delta\neq\bot
, and in both cases
\bot\preceq\Delta
.
∎

Lemma C.11.

Let
P^{\prime}\preceq P
, if
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P^{\prime},R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta^{\prime}
then
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta
for some
\Delta
such that
\Delta^{\prime}\preceq\Delta
.

Proof.

Consider the case in which
\Delta^{\prime}\neq\bot
.
We proceed by induction on the rules for
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P^{\prime},R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta^{\prime}
.

Rules
OQOp
,
OQMeas
,
ParL
and
ParL
are trivial, since the rule is applicable for any process
P
.

Consider
Input
, then
P^{\prime}=(c!v+P_{1}^{\prime})\parallel P_{2}^{\prime}\setminus D
and
R=c?x.R_{1}+R_{2}
with
c\notin D
.
Moreover,
\pi=\epsilon
and
\Delta^{\prime}=\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P_{2}^{\prime}\setminus D,R_{1}[v/x]}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
.
Notice that
P^{\prime}
can only be the refinement of
P=(c!v+P_{1})\parallel P_{2}\setminus D
for
P_{i}^{\prime}\preceq P_{i}
.
Then the result holds by applying
Input
for obtaining
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\longsquiggly_{\pi}\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P_{2}\setminus D,R_{1}[v/x]}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}
.

The proof for
Output
is the same, and follows from the fact that
Q^{\prime}\preceq Q
implies
Q^{\prime}[v/x]\preceq Q[v/x]
for any
v
and
x
.

Consider
process
and assume
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P^{\prime}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Delta^{\prime}
.
Then, by
C.10
,
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\rightarrow\Delta
with
\Delta^{\prime}\preceq\Delta
.
We can thus apply
process
to derive
O[\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}]\longsquiggly_{\diamond}O[\Delta]
, and
O[\Delta^{\prime}]\preceq O[\Delta]
holds by definition.

Consider
Congr
and assume
P^{\prime}\equiv Q^{\prime}
,
R\equiv_{O}S
,
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,Q^{\prime},S}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Theta^{\prime}
and
\Theta^{\prime}\equiv_{O}\Delta^{\prime}
.
From
C.9
,
P\equiv Q
such that
Q^{\prime}\preceq Q
, and thus, by induction hypothesis,
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,Q,S}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Theta
with
\Theta^{\prime}\preceq\Theta
.
Hence, from
C.9
and by definition of
\preceq
, a distribution
\Delta
exists such that
\Theta\equiv\Delta
and
\Delta\preceq\Delta^{\prime}
.
We can thus apply
Congr
to derive
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta
.

Finally, consider
\Delta^{\prime}=\bot
.
Then,
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta
either for
\Delta=\bot
or for some
\Delta\neq\bot
, and in both cases
\bot\preceq\Delta
.
∎

See
4.12

Proof.

By
C.11
and decomposability of
\longsquiggly_{\pi}
.
∎

Appendix D
Proofs of Section
4.4

See
4.14

Proof.

To prove the first point, suppose
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\sim_{cs}\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\sigma,Q,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}
, with
\rho,\sigma\in\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{q},\tilde{p}}
and
\tilde{p}\vdash P
,
\tilde{p}\vdash Q
. For any superoperator
\mathcal{TS}(\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{q}})

we can construct a context

O[{\,\cdot\,}]=[{\,\cdot\,}]\parallel\mathcal{E}\!\left({\tilde{q}}\right)\!.(a!0\parallel c!\tilde{q})

where
a
is a fresh channel. We know that
O[\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}]
and
O[\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\sigma,Q,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}]
are bisimilar, and
O[\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}]
can evolve in
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{q}}\!\left(\rho\right)\!,P,a!0\parallel c!\tilde{q}\parallel\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}
.
Then
O[\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\sigma,Q,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}]
must evolve in
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{q}}\!\left(\sigma\right)\!,Q,a!0\parallel c!\tilde{q}\parallel\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}
, because it must match the
\downarrow_{a}
barb, and
a
is fresh. So we have that

\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{q}}\!\left(\rho\right)\!,P,a!0\parallel c!\tilde{q}\parallel\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\sim_{cs}\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{q}}\!\left(\sigma\right)\!,Q,a!0\parallel c!\tilde{q}\parallel\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}

and from this it follows

\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{q}}\!\left(\rho\right)\!,P,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\sim_{cs}\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{q}}\!\left(\sigma\right)\!,Q,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}

simply by contradiction: if there was a context capable of distinguishing
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{q}}\!\left(\rho\right)\!,P,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}
from
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{q}}\!\left(\rho\right)\!,P,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}
then there would be a context able to distinguish also
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{q}}\!\left(\rho\right)\!,P,a!0\parallel c!\tilde{q}\parallel\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}
from
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{q}}\!\left(\sigma\right)\!,Q,a!0\parallel c!\tilde{q}\parallel\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}
.

To prove the second point we proceed by contradiction, supposing
\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}\sim_{cs}\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\sigma,Q,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}
and
tr_{\tilde{p}}(\rho)\neq tr_{\tilde{p}}(\sigma)
, with
\rho,\sigma\in\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{q},\tilde{p}}
and
\tilde{p}\vdash P
,
\tilde{p}\vdash Q
.
If
\tr_{\tilde{p}}\left(\rho\right)\neq\tr_{\tilde{p}}\left(\sigma\right)
, then there exists a measurement
M_{\tilde{q}}=\{M_{1},\ldots,M_{m}\}
that distinguishes them, i.e. such that
p_{m}(\tr_{\tilde{p}}\left(\rho\right))=tr(M_{m}\tr_{\tilde{p}}\left(\rho\right)M_{m}^{\dagger})\neq tr(M_{m}\tr_{\tilde{p}}\left(\sigma\right)M_{m}^{\dagger})=p_{m}(\tr_{\tilde{p}}\left(\sigma\right))
for some
m
. But for
A.3
, the same probabilities arise also from the measurement
M_{\tilde{q}\tilde{p}}=\{M_{1}\otimes I_{\tilde{p}},\ldots,M_{m}\otimes I_{\tilde{p}}\}
, that can therefore distinguish the whole state
\rho
from
\sigma
. So, taken the context

\displaystyle O[{\,\cdot\,}]=

\displaystyle[{\,\cdot\,}]\parallel M(\tilde{q}\rhd{}x).

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}_{\tilde{q}}\parallel\textbf{if }x=1\textbf{ then }c_{1}!0\textbf{ else }\ldots

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\textbf{if }x=m-1\textbf{ then }c_{m-1}!0\textbf{ else }c_{m}!0

where
c_{1}\ldots c_{m}
are fresh channels, we have that
O[\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}]
should be bisimilar to
O[\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\sigma,Q,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}]
.
But

O[\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,P,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}]\longsquiggly_{r}\sum_{m}p_{m}(\rho)\bullet\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho_{m},P,\mathbf{0}\parallel c_{m}!0}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}

and
O[\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\sigma,Q,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}]
can perform only the transition

O[\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\sigma,Q,\mathbf{0}}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}]\longsquiggly_{r}\sum_{m}p_{m}(\sigma)\bullet\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\sigma_{m},Q,\mathbf{0}\parallel c_{m}!0}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}

to match the barbs, but we know that
p_{m}(\rho)\neq p_{m}(\sigma)
for at least one
m
.
∎

In order to prove
4.16
, we need an additional lemma.

Lemma D.1.

Assume that
\Delta
is a distribution such that
\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\Delta\right)
is well-defined. Then, for
O[{\,\cdot\,}]
we have

O[\Delta]\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta^{\prime}\text{ if and only if }O[\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\Delta\right)]\longsquiggly_{\pi}\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\Delta^{\prime}\right)

Proof.

We will prove that, for any
\mathcal{C}\in\mathit{Conf}_{\!\!\bot}

\overline{\mathcal{C}}\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta^{\prime}\text{ if and only if }\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\overline{\mathcal{C}}\right)\longsquiggly_{\pi}\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\Delta^{\prime}\right)

from which the desired lemma follows easily by linearity.

We will proceed by induction on
\overline{\mathcal{C}}\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta^{\prime}
. First, if
\mathcal{C}=\bot
, then the property follows from the definitions of
O[\bot]
and
\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\bot\right)
. Otherwise, notice that
disc(\tilde{q})
is a deadlock process that cannot take part in any synchronization. The only interesting base cases are
QOp
,
QMeas
,
OQOp
and
OQMeas
, when the process (or the observer) modifies the quantum state. We will deal only with the process, as the proof for observer is the same. All the other rules of
\rightarrow
and
\longsquiggly
are trivial, as they do not modify the quantum state.

Assume that

\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\rho,(\mathcal{E}\!\left(\tilde{x}\right)\!.P+Q\parallel\mathbf{0}_{\tilde{q}}),R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{x}}\!\left(\rho\right)\!,(P\parallel\mathbf{0}_{\tilde{q}}),R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}

with
\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{x}}\!\left(\rho\right)\!=(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\tilde{y}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\tilde{q}})(\rho)
, where
\tilde{y}
are all the qubits not in
\tilde{x}
nor in
\tilde{q}
, and
\mathcal{I}_{\tilde{y}}
is the identity superoperator on said qubits.
Then we also have

\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\rho\right),(\mathcal{E}\!\left(\tilde{x}\right)\!.P+Q),R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{x}}\!\left(\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\rho\right)\right)\!,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}

with
\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{x}}\!\left(\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\rho\right)\right)\!=(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\tilde{y}})(\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\rho\right))
.
From
A.3
, we have

(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\tilde{y}})(\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\rho\right))=\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left((\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\tilde{y}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\tilde{q}})(\rho)\right)

and we conclude

\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{x}}\!\left(\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\rho\right)\right)\!,P,R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}=\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\overline{\mathmbox{\big\langle\hskip-4.4pt\big\langle}{\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{x}}\!\left(\rho\right)\!,(P\parallel\mathbf{0}_{\tilde{q}}),R}\mathmbox{\big\rangle\hskip-4.4pt\big\rangle}}\right)

The other direction is similar.

The
QMeas
case is also similar:
A.3
implies
tr\left(\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{\tilde{x},m}\!\left(\rho\right)\!\right)\right)=tr\left(\mathcal{M}_{\tilde{x},m}\!\left(\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\rho\right)\right)\!\right)
, so
\Delta^{\prime}
and
\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\Delta^{\prime}\right)
have the same probabilities.
∎

We can now prove that distribution bisimilarity is closed for additional discarded qubits.

See
4.16

Proof.

We need to show that

\mathcal{R}=\left\{(\Delta,\Theta)\mid\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\Delta\right)\sim_{cs}\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\Theta\right)\right\}

is a distribution bisimulation. Notice that if
\Sigma\vdash\Delta,\Theta
, then
\Sigma\setminus\tilde{q}\vdash\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\Delta\right),\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\Theta\right)
.

For the first condition, observe that
\Delta\downarrow^{p}_{b}
if and only if
\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\Delta\right)\downarrow^{p}_{b}
, since the partial trace operation is linear and the process
\mathbf{0}_{\tilde{q}}
does not express any barb.

For the second condition, it is sufficient to notice that
\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(O[\Delta]\right)=O[\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\Delta\right)]
for any context
O[{\,\cdot\,}]
. Then, we have that if
O[\Delta]\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Delta^{\prime}
, then from
D.1
we know that
\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(O[\Delta]\right)=O[\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\Delta\right)]\longsquiggly_{\pi}\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\Delta^{\prime}\right)
, and then
O[\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\Theta\right)]\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Theta^{\prime\prime}\sim_{cs}\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\Delta^{\prime}\right)
and
O[\Theta]\longsquiggly_{\pi}\Theta^{\prime}
such that
\Theta^{\prime\prime}=\tr_{\tilde{q}}\left(\Theta^{\prime}\right)
. The third condition is symmetric.
∎

See
4.19

Proof.

Soundness follows from compatibility, proven in
C.7
, as shown in
(
Pous and Sangiorgi 2011
)
.
∎
